# Tartaria Realms — Version Ledger

`VERSION.md` is the **single source of truth for the version number.**
Read it before every native build; advance it on every OTA.

## Scheme — `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (semantic versioning)

- **MAJOR** — a big / milestone jump (new engine lineage, major rework).
  Bumping MAJOR resets MINOR and PATCH to 0.
- **MINOR** — a significant feature wave. Bumping MINOR resets PATCH to 0.
- **PATCH** — **incremented on every OTA** since the last MINOR. So
  `3.2.17` = MAJOR 3, the 2nd significant feature wave, 17 OTAs after it.

## The two-version rule (important — do not break this)

There are two version numbers and only one moves on an OTA:

1. **Logical version** (this file + `app/buildInfo.ts` `APP_SEMVER`) —
   ships in the JS bundle, so the About screen can display it and it
   advances on **every OTA**. The PATCH digit lives here.
2. **Native / runtime version** (`app.json` `version`, with
   `runtimeVersion.policy = "appVersion"`) — this *is* the OTA runtime.
   It changes **only when a native APK/AAB is built**, and is stamped
   with the current logical version at that moment.
   **Never bump `app.json` `version` on an OTA** — it would change the
   runtime out from under installed devices and break OTA delivery.

### Workflow

- **Per OTA push:** PATCH += 1 → update `DISPLAY_VERSION` in
  `app/buildInfo.ts` (the character-select / About game version) in the same
  edit as the `OTA_BUILD_ID` bump. (The old `APP_SEMVER` field never survived
  the buildInfo rework — `DISPLAY_VERSION` is the carrier now.) Log rows here
  are for MINOR/MAJOR moves and native builds, not every PATCH.
- **Significant feature:** MINOR += 1, PATCH = 0.
- **Native APK/AAB build:** stamp `app.json` `version` with the value in
  `NEXT`, then continue OTAs from there.

---

## State

- **Current native / runtime version (`app.json`):** `2.4.1`
  (the live Google Play internal-test build; dev lineage)
- **NEXT — value to stamp on the next native build:** whatever
  `DISPLAY_VERSION` reads at build time (`4.29.145` as of this write).
- **Current logical version (`DISPLAY_VERSION`):** `4.29.145` — REACTIVATED at
  `4.28.3` on 2026-07-26 after freezing at `4.1.0` (OTA-602; see the catch-up
  ledger below), then PATCH +1 per OTA through the 993–1016 run.

> Builds before `3.0.0` (the `2.x` series) predate this ledger and were
> numbered ad hoc. The scheme starts cleanly at the `3.0.0` promotion.

| 4.29.104 -> 4.29.105 | 2026-08-08-1172 | OTA | ⚠ MEMORY WARNINGS, APP-STATE CHURN, AND A FREEZE DETECTOR. Owner, after a hard lock on an iPhone this log could not explain: *"add in memory warning codes to the log so you can track them, whatever debug information you need. add that to whatever you can immediately put in as an OTA and push it. I want that done now."* **⚠ WHAT THE REPORT PROVED, AND WHERE IT RAN OUT.** The qwen-watchdog ticked every ~10s **straight through the freeze** (12:29:58 → 12:31:27) and a save landed at 12:31:02 — so **the JS thread was alive the whole time the screen was dead.** That rules out an infinite loop and every pure-logic suspect, including both levers shipped that same day (the dodge bar cannot even render outside combat, and he was in an outpost with `enemies=0`). Having ruled those out, the log had nothing left to say: no record of a tap arriving, none of the screen painting, none of memory pressure. Three holes, three instruments. ⚠⚠ **NOTHING IN THE APP LISTENED FOR `memoryWarning` — verified by grep, not assumed.** On the platform the bug was filed from, that is the highest-value signal available: iOS warns before it stalls the app and again before it kills it, and *frozen-but-alive is what memory pressure looks like from the inside.* **Five instruments.** (1) The **memory-warning line** carries an **ordinal** — the count IS the severity, because iOS escalates — plus the gap since the last one and the context that indicts the suspect: qwen status, **reload count**, save KB. (2) The **freeze watch runs TWO clocks**: `setTimeout` is serviced by the JS thread alone, `requestAnimationFrame` is driven by the native frame callback and stops when the *render* side stops. ⚠ Neither clock alone can tell a frozen screen from a wedged engine; **the pair can** — which is the exact question this report left open and I had to ask the owner by hand. ⚠ The two are measured differently on purpose: the JS gap is **net** of the sample interval (or the sampler indicts itself every tick), the frame gap is **raw** (rAF should fire at ~16ms whatever the sample rate). ⚠ It judges only while foregrounded — a backgrounded app rightly stops painting, and a detector that cries wolf is one nobody reads — and logs on the **edge**, not once per sample. (3) The **app-state trail** records **every** transition including `inactive`: on iOS that is the evidence, not the noise — the freeze log showed three `active` bounces ~350ms apart, which is why the watchdog appeared to contradict itself (*"holding revival"* then reinitializing a third of a second later). (4) The **tap breadcrumb**, logged **before any handler** — that ordering is the whole signal: tap logged with no parser line means the engine hung; **no tap line at all means the screen was frozen.** (5) **Reload timing**, ms and resulting status, because six attempts went `idle → idle` and nothing recorded whether they were expensive-and-working or expensive-and-futile. ⚠⚠ **DIAGNOSTICS ONLY, AND THE FIX IS HELD BACK DELIBERATELY.** The same log shows a **real defect**: `AppState` `'active'` **wipes the Qwen backoff ladder and immediately kicks a ~400MB model reload**, and iOS fires `'active'` for a notification banner, a Control Center pull or a peek at the app switcher — so **three of the six reloads in that window were incidental twitches, not the player returning.** OTA-1084's own comment says *"kicking a ~400MB context load from the background is guaranteed wasted work"*; iOS walks straight through that rule. The fix is **not** in this OTA on purpose — **instrument first, then fix**, or the next device log cannot say which change moved it. The current behaviour is **pinned by test** so the hold is visible and the change cannot land unannounced. ⚠ Every listener is guarded, the watch is idempotent across re-hydrate, and instrumentation can never throw into a press handler or break the bug-report export — a diagnostic that fails when things are already going wrong is worse than none. New suite ota1172RuntimePressure (35 tests). |

I had to hard stop the app."* **⚠ THE CRASH IS THE MORE DIAGNOSTIC OF THE TWO,** and the log dates it to the second: `12:46:27.037` watchdog reinitializing (#2) → `12:46:27.931` *investigate the floor* → `12:46:28.008` cognitive (70ms) → **gone**, voice engine re-initialising 10s later on a fresh launch. A ~400MB load racing an inference is exactly that shape. **Four changes, each defensible on its own terms.** (1) ⚠⚠ **THE MODEL LOAD TAKES THE NATIVE-ML LOCK — it never did.** Completion took it (OTA-459's Tensor G5 SIGSEGV), release took it (OTA-1123); the **~400MB context load, larger than either, was the one native call going in unserialized**, so a reload could land on top of a Kokoro synth and a Qwen completion at the same instant. We hold a ~400MB context and have a `dispose()` for it — **Qwen switched off is enormously better than the app dying**, and narration degrades to templates while the game keeps playing. ⚠ The watchdog is deliberately **not** suppressed: release under pressure, recover when it lifts. (3) ⚠⚠ **AN iOS TWITCH NO LONGER BUYS A 400MB RELOAD** — the defect OTA-1172 deliberately held back. `AppState` `'active'` fires on iOS for a notification banner, a Control Center pull or a peek at the app switcher, and it **wiped the backoff ladder and kicked an immediate reload**; the freeze log caught three, each ~350ms after a *"holding revival"* line. A genuine `'background'` must now precede an `'active'` before either resets. It stands down, says so **once**, and lifts on a real put-away-and-return. ⚠⚠ **THE OTA-1172 HOLD IS OVER, and the reversal is on the record.** That OTA shipped instruments only so the next log would measure the bug untouched. Overtaken: **this report arrived still on 1194, so the instruments never ran**, and the symptom went from a freeze to a lost session. Sitting on a plausible mitigation for methodological purity while the owner loses runs is the wrong trade. ⚠ **The cause remains a HYPOTHESIS** — no memory warning has yet been *observed* — and the source says so rather than asserting it. Pinned by a test that imports over a populated store and asserts the originals are byte-identical. ⚠ **The envelope is built for truncation, because on this app that is the EXPECTED failure:** a save runs to 800,000 chars (SAFE_BLOB_CHARS), far past the 25,000 at which TitleScreen already chunks logs since *"most chat clients silently truncate larger pastes"* (OTA-023). So the export carries a declared character count and an FNV-1a checksum, and the END marker is written last — a clipped paste loses it first. | 4.29.112 -> 4.29.113 | 2026-08-09-1180 | OTA | ⚠⚠ A FAILED MODEL LOAD WAS BEING RECORDED AS AN INIT SUCCESS — AND IT WAS WIPING THE CRASH GUARD. **MEASURED (owner report, 2026-08-09, HAL build 1202 — the owner runs the HAL line):** header claims a healthy init while every other signal says the model never loaded — `Boot stage: qwen:done`, `Last init success: 03:28:28`, `Crash count: 0`, yet `Model contexts — Opened: 0`, `⚠⚠ MEMORY WARNING #1 — qwen='failed'`, and `arbiter: template (reason=qwen-not-ready)` throughout. **Cause:** `bootQwen()` states it in its own comment — *"qwen.initialize() swallows errors and sets its own internal status to 'failed' rather than throwing"* — then sets `qwenStatus: 'failed'` and **returns normally**, so App.tsx's `.then()` ran on the failure path and called `setStage('qwen:done')` + `markMLInitSucceeded()`. ⚠⚠ **NOT COSMETIC:** `markMLInitSucceeded()` deliberately wipes `KEY_CRASH_COUNT` and `KEY_DISABLED` (arb124 — a real success proves the device can load the model). THE EXILE: the PARDON exists, on a courier who died nine months ago carrying it to you · the exile rolls are complete and your name was never entered — there was no sentence, only a man in a chair (FORGOTTEN) · the MAGISTRATE who signed came down after the fall and holds court in the dark for an audience of nobody (fight). THE CALLING: a civic BEACON, automated, calling since 1530 because nobody was left to switch it off · a CHOIR — nine others heard it too, and the vigil is mostly company for whoever walks in next · the hum is aware, patient, and building itself a body out of salvage, and it wants the part of you that could hear it (fight). THE RECORD: a PREDECESSOR wrote it first, better, four hundred pages, and died at the desk unsigned · a READER three days deeper is already carrying hand-copies of YOUR notes and corrects your northern survey before asking your name · a CENSOR that has taken the words off a century of other people's paper turns and looks at your satchel (fight). **Same machine throughout:** dealt from the character's identity (stable per save, all three reachable), gated identically to the Missing trail (all five of that motive's beats seen + 3 Cores + the next Lost Capital arrival), one of the three a boss whose keepsake and closing beats land on the KILL — so a fled fight re-offers at the next Capital instead of losing the ending forever. `player.motiveResolved` stores it; **`missingResolved` stays authoritative for The Missing, so a save that finished that trail before this OTA needs no migration and reads exactly as it did.** ⚠⚠ THE SUITE CAUGHT A LATENT BUG IN THE SHIPPED CODE: `resolutionBlock` filled `{name}` into the arrival lines, the defeat lines and the epilogue — but **NOT into the keepsake**. Invisible for two years because the three Missing keepsakes happen to carry no token; the first authored keepsake that used one ("the {name} house's field ledger") would have shipped the raw braces inside an item the player keeps forever. Fixed at the root, and the audit now pins every field of every block against the token. | 4.29.157 -> 4.29.158 | 2026-08-12-1230 | OTA | ⚠⚠ THE NAME LINE — TWO HOLES IN A FILTER NOBODY HAD RUN, AND A FULL INJECTION AUDIT OF EVERYWHERE THE NAME TRAVELS. Owner: *"are we able to stop people from entering emojis in any special characters in the name line?"* and then *"just so I'm sure there's no way anything put in that line comes out as anything other than a name... there's no way to inject anything in there or do any malicious code."* Two questions, and the second one is the reason this row is long. **⚠ THE ANSWER TO THE FIRST WAS "MOSTLY, ALREADY"** — OTA-635 has stripped the name to letters + space/hyphen/apostrophe since it shipped, capped it at 24 (the voice reads it aloud; someone typed 175 J's), and re-prompts when nothing usable is left. Emoji, digits, punctuation and zalgo were all already handled. **But the filter had never been RUN against real input, and running it found two faults pointing in opposite directions.** **(1) "LETTERS" IS A MUCH BIGGER CATEGORY THAN IT SOUNDS.** Mathematical script, mathematical bold, double-struck, fraktur, fullwidth, circled, superscript and roman-numeral characters are all Unicode category **L**, so a letters-only filter waved every one of them straight through: `𝓥𝓮𝓻𝓫𝓪𝓵`, `Ｖｅｒｂａｌ`, `ᴠᴇʀʙᴀʟ` all survived intact — unreadable in most fonts and unpronounceable by the synth. Fixed by switching the normalisation from **NFC to NFKC** — the compatibility fold, which maps 𝓥 / Ｖ / Ⓥ / Ⅴ / ⱽ all back to a plain V. **That is one letter of difference in the source and it closes the entire class.** ⚠ It also FIXES two names that were being destroyed rather than passed: `Ⓥⓔⓡⓑⓐⓛ` used to clean to `''` and get refused; it now reads `Verbal`. The one block NFKC cannot reach is Latin small-caps / phonetic extensions (no compatibility decomposition), folded by hand in a 25-entry table. **⚠⚠ (2) AND THE WORSE ONE, IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION: COMBINING MARKS WERE BEING STRIPPED AS "NOT LETTERS".** In Devanagari, Thai, Hebrew and Arabic those marks are not decoration — **the virama in `नमस्ते` JOINS two letters**, and removing it silently broke a legitimate name into `नमसत`. Same for Thai vowel marks and Hebrew niqqud. Marks are now KEPT, and zalgo is handled by **bounding the run at two marks per base character** rather than banning the category: real scripts never stack more than two, and banning them outright is exactly what broke Devanagari. A quantity rule, not a category one. **⚠ Also closed: the hyphen/apostrophe loophole.** Both are kept so `Mary-Jane` and `O'Brien` survive — which meant `Rob--------` and `''''Rob''''` cleaned to themselves, every character being on the keep list. Runs collapse to one and a name can no longer open or close on punctuation. **⚠⚠ AND THE STRIP NO LONGER HAPPENS IN SILENCE.** Type `Verbal123`, become `Verbal`, and nothing anywhere admitted the game had edited your answer — the first place a player found out was the character sheet, where it reads as a bug rather than a rule. The Arbiter now says it once, in his own register, and **only when the text actually differs** — ordinary whitespace tidying is not worth a line about nothing. **⚠⚠ THE INJECTION AUDIT — MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED.** Every sink the name could reach, checked by grep across `app/` and `App.tsx`: **(a) NO code-execution sinks exist anywhere in the app** — `eval(`, `new Function(`, `innerHTML` and `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` return **zero hits**; **(b) NO WebView** anywhere in the tree; **(c)** the only `Linking.openURL` calls open fixed URLs or a `mailto:` whose subject and body are `encodeURIComponent`-wrapped, so header injection is closed; **(d)** the name renders through React Native `<Text>`, which has no markup path — there is no HTML parser in the render path to inject into; **(e)** it is stored via AsyncStorage as `JSON.stringify` — **no SQL, no shell, and no filesystem path is ever built from it**; **(f) ⚠ it never reaches the LLM prompt at all** — `LlmContext` carries no name field and `formatPlayerStats` emits only HP / stamina / AC / corruption / effects, so not even prompt-influence of the local narrator is on the table. The name is interpolated into narration prose (`You are ${playerName} of the ${raceName}…`), but that is a JS template producing a plain string bound for `<Text>`. **It is a string, and it is only ever a string.** The sanitizer exists so the name is READABLE and SPEAKABLE — not because anything downstream is unsafe with odd characters in it, and the comment in `playerName.ts` now says so explicitly so no future reader mistakes it for a security boundary. New suite ota1230NameHygiene (9), written as a before/after table — the fancy-lookalike folds, the joined scripts kept whole, the bounded zalgo, the punctuation loophole, the length cap applied AFTER the fold (so 200 fullwidth chars cannot slip past by counting differently), and the store's admission line. The original ota635 suite (`playerNameHygiene`, 6) passes unchanged.

Without a mark, answering it means leaving the picker, opening Inventory, comparing, and coming back, per piece. ⚠ **COMPARED AGAINST REAL CATALOG NUMBERS, NOT PARSED PROSE** — armor on `acBonus` from the ARMOR table against the piece in the SAME slot, weapons on average damage-dice against the equipped main hand, both from the tables combat rolls on. It compares ONE axis per kind, so it means "better on the number that decides whether you get hit / how hard you hit" — not "better in every respect". **Overstating it would be worse than having no mark, because the player would stop reading the rows.** Sort is STABLE and consumed rows SINK rather than jumping, so a half-looted room does not reshuffle under the thumb between taps. **⚠⚠ (2) SELL ALL COMMON GEAR — ONE BUTTON, AND THREE TRAPS DISARMED.** Owner: *"some games have a sell all scrap button... that seems to be my most sold items. those are the ones marked beige in my inventory correct?"* Beige is correct (`#c9a86a` in both rarity colour maps) — **but the button as literally described would have sold things he badly wants.** ⚠⚠ **COMMON IS NOT A JUNK TIER, IT IS THE DEFAULT TIER:** from his own device log, `Scrap Metal (Common)`, `Aether Dust (Common)`, `Worn Tartarian Coin (Common)`, plus Trail Rations and Bioluminescent Fungus. A literal "sell all Common" empties the crafting stock and the food in one tap — hence **GEAR**, weapons and armor only, drawn from the WEAPONS/ARMOR catalogs themselves. ⚠⚠ **AND BEIGE IS THE `default:` BRANCH** of that colour switch, so it renders Common AND anything unrecognised; selecting on the colour would sweep the unknowns, so this keys on `rarity === 'Common'` explicitly and leaves a rarity-less item alone. ⚠ **A CRUCIBLE-FORGED PIECE CAN SIT AT COMMON** while carrying real work — excluded, because rarity is a poor proxy for effort and the one thing a bulk sell must never do is spend something the player built. **ONE button, not the two he first sketched** (weapons/armor): splitting means two taps for one intention and two more things to read on a phone, and the per-item rows are right there for exceptions. ⚠ **THE COUNT AND TOTAL ARE ON THE BUTTON'S FACE, not just in the confirm** — a bulk action whose size you only learn after committing to look is one people stop trusting — and the confirm names what is deliberately NOT in the sweep, because a boundary the player cannot see has to be taken on trust. Equipped exclusion is INHERITED from the existing sell list, which already filters by INSTANCE ID (OTA-664), so a spare copy of an equipped item's name stays sellable. The plan is RE-COMPUTED at fire time against the live list, and each row still goes through `sellToVendor` individually, so every piece takes the same price, log line and standing effect it would have taken sold by hand — **a shortcut for the taps, not for the rules.** **⚠⚠ (3) THE REFUSALS STOP ADVERTISING A VERB THAT WILL TURN YOU AWAY.** All eight scene-feature refusals ended with "(Try SALVAGE.)" unconditionally — OTA-137 made it universal so a hoarder would learn the verb, which was right. **But measured against the shipped pools, `sign`, `arch` and `wall` match NO salvage pool**, so on those nouns the advice sent the player off to do the one thing that provably cannot help: the same defect the contract refusal used to commit when it blamed travel for an empty board. Now TWO sets of eight, chosen by a new PURE, DETERMINISTIC `hasSalvageYield` (it mirrors `rollSalvagePool`'s first two decisions — the sigil head-noun gate, then `pickPool` — and nothing after, because everything past that point changes WHAT you get, never WHETHER there is anything). The scenery set promises nothing and names INVESTIGATE, which does work on scenery and returns lore. ⚠ **Both sets stay EIGHT lines deep** — the variety is why a hoarder does not read the same sentence forty times, and halving it to make room for the new case would trade one player-facing problem for another. New suite ota1232GatherAndBulkSell (16). ⚠ HONEST LIMIT: the full MERGE of the take and salvage popups into one — which is where this discussion started — is NOT in this OTA. The sort, the icons and the ★ are, and they are the half the owner described wanting; the physical merge needs SalvageModal's results phase, its elevation filter and two tutorial beats moved without loss, and doing that badly in the loot flow immediately after fixing bugs in the loot flow is a poor trade. Staged next, with the tutorial + refusal copy pass that has to land alongside it.

So an open-empty picker SAYS the room is picked clean and WAITS: that player needs an explanation, not a dismissal. New validator rule `negated_command`: scan backward from the verb, stop at a coordinating conjunction (negation does not scope across "so"/"but"/"and", so *"I have nothing SO I will attack"* is still an attack), window 4 — **measured, not picked**: the longest real distance is 3 (*"I do not want to sleep"*). **⚠⚠ SECOND FIND, FROM READING THE LIST:** `where` and `when` were **missing** from the `ask` synonyms while `what`/`who`/`how`/`why` were all present — so a question that named an action performed it. *"where can I sleep"* slept eight hours; *"when does the shop open"* opened the chest. The wh-word sits at index 0 and the scan takes the FIRST minimum-distance hit, so restoring the two words is the whole fix; **no new mechanism**. Corpus `question` 14/15 → **15/15**, dictionary-domain hit rate 98.3% → **99.2%**. **⚠⚠ THIRD, AND THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED THIS INERT:** a demoted parse falls through to the **Qwen resolver**, whose entire job is to find an actionable verb in the sentence — handing it *"do not open the chest"* is asking for `open` back. Every other demotion means "I could not work out what you wanted" and belongs there; this one means the player was perfectly clear and the clear thing was DON'T. It is answered in the store and **stopped before the fallback gate**, and the test pins the ORDERING, because the ordering IS the fix. **Same shape as the N2 mistake earlier this session: a change that looked right and never fired.** ⚠ **`describeIssues` HAS ITS FIRST CALLER since OTA-205** — ~1000 OTAs of dead code; demoted parses had always fallen through to the generic refusal. | 4.29.198 -> 4.29.199 | 2026-08-15-1276 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE LOG CANNOT SHOW THE FREEZE — AND EVERY DIAGNOSIS THAT READ ITS LAST LINE AS THE FREEZE POINT, INCLUDING MINE, WAS STANDING ON SAND.** The owner corrected the theory and the correction IS the diagnosis: *"I always froze mid game... just cycling room to room inside the outpost scavenging"* — **not** while copying logs (that was the after-action report, which is all OTA-1275's thrash ever was). **⚠⚠ FREEZE #2 GAVE THE SIGNATURE:** *"it froze every single tap button... but the text box in the middle was still scrollable so touch input still worked."* In React Native, **ScrollView scrolling is driven natively and survives a dead JS thread; `onPress` needs JS**. Buttons dead + scroll alive = **the JS thread is wedged**. (The Android nav bar returning on swipe-up is a system gesture — it proves nothing about JS, though it was a sharp thing to notice.) **⚠⚠ AND A WEDGE TAKES THE ENTIRE INSTRUMENT SUITE WITH IT.** `appendLogToDisk` batches into `pendingLogLines` and drains on a **promise chain** — promises need the JS thread, so **the final lines before a freeze die in memory and never reach disk**. The freeze sampler is a `setTimeout`. And Clock A, commented as *"driven by the NATIVE frame callback"*, is `requestAnimationFrame` — **a JS timer in RN** (`JSTimers.js:257`). All three die together. That is why `Freeze watch: no stalls seen` printed straight through a hard freeze, and why the disk log ends at the last successful **FLUSH** rather than at the freeze. **I replayed the wrong scene twice off that cutoff.** **THE FIX IS AN INSTRUMENT WRITTEN AHEAD OF THE WEDGE, NOT A MEASUREMENT TAKEN AFTER IT:** `stampLiveBreadcrumb` — one tiny single-key write, **never batched, never chained, no read-modify-write of the 400KB log** — issued the moment an action starts, on **every UI tap and every player action**, carrying screen + room. Cleared on an orderly exit (arb126's discipline), so a breadcrumb that **survives to the next boot means the process died while live** — precisely the swipe-kill he has to perform. The report now prints *what the app was actually doing last*, where, and when — and **says out loud that the disk log's tail is unreliable**, so the next reader is stopped from making my mistake. ⚠ **THIS IS NOT A FIX FOR THE FREEZE.** It is the instrument that will NAME it next time. The loop itself is still unfound — every `while` in the scavenge/room-cycle path was reviewed and all are bounded. **Recorded as unfound rather than guessed at.** New suite ota1276FreezeForensics (10), including pins that both freeze clocks are JS-thread timers and that the breadcrumb deliberately avoids the batching chain. ota1172's tap-breadcrumb window widened with its reason. 815 suites / 7621 tests. ⚠ STILL GOLEM-ONLY. |

The other **eleven are deliberately left alone rather than forced** — most are in the molten south where the art has few distinct structures and the nearest candidate was beyond the cap or already claimed. Forcing them would park a name on a building belonging to something else, which is worse than a name on open ground; the Black Reach is the clearest case, being a hole in the world with nothing built there to sit on. ⚠⚠ **PINS MOVE WITH THE NAMES.** Event "?" and contract "◆" markers are keyed by CELL rather than by location id, so a new module-load reverse map (canonical cell → owning location) lets them take the same nudge; a cell with no owner falls through to the grid position, which is right — there is no silhouette for it to sit on. Without this a contract pin would have sat up to a tile from the very name it belongs to: two marks for one place, in two places. ⚠ Label clearance tightened 16 px → 9 px so a name reads as ATTACHED to its silhouette, and the candidate rings went 3 → 5 after the nudge boxed in Reclaimer's Stake against the western edge — it had fallen back to an overlapping slot, and more distance options are free. **37 labels, zero collisions** (31 below, 2 above, 2 left, 2 right). ⚠ `atlasLabelConflicts` was checking the TRUE anchors while the solver avoided the VISUAL ones — checker and solver testing two different maps, and the checker testing the one nobody sees. | 4.29.255 -> 4.29.256 | 2026-08-18-1362 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE SHARED ROLL OF THE FALLEN — VALIDATOR + MERGE RULES (step 1 of the shared-dead feature).** Owner's design: a handful of players share their dead. Your fallen walk in their wastes as Hollowed revenants, theirs in yours, the pool grows and the world gets heavier; putting one down writes it to a SEPARATE roll naming the player it came from. ⚠ **GOLEM ONLY** — owner: *"this is a golem line only feature… don't push this to HAL until we can test it on Golem with myself and another player I give the golem APK to."* Not ported to HAL or steam. **The gameplay half already existed** (`fallenRevenants.ts` builds the revenant, scales it off the dead character's kills and the living player's hpMax, spawns it on quiet wild ground, hands back the real gear). This OTA is the door that decides what a record from ANOTHER PHONE may be — built FIRST, before any transport, because it is what protects the save. ⚠⚠ **THE THREAT IS NOT A VIRUS — IT IS ITEM INJECTION.** Owner asked about "a hash check to check for viruses". There is no virus vector (the payload is JSON that only becomes a FallenHero, never code), and a hash proves only that bytes arrived intact while a signature proves only who sent them — NEITHER says the data is sane, because whoever writes a hostile record writes its hash too. The live hole: `reconstructFallenPiece` spreads a gear object wholesale into a real InventoryItem, and `FallenGearPiece` is `Omit<InventoryItem,'id'|'quantity'>` — every field. A hand-edited record could carry a `golemCore` worth half a trained golem, a Legendary rarity, fused `uniqueStats`, or a `quest` tag that lands in the save as undroppable — permanent the moment you reclaim the revenant's weapon. **New `app/engine/fallenLedger.ts`:** allowlist reconstruction (every field read by name, nothing spread; golemCore / uniqueStats / coatings / stolen / selfCrafted / reserved* / materializing all dropped by construction), numeric clamps (kills ≤ 500, acBonus ≤ 20, stat bonuses ≤ ±5 and only real stats), bounded + control-char-scrubbed strings, quest-LOCK tags stripped, future-dated corpses refused (they would never age out of the pool), and the sender may NOT declare its own corpse avenged. **Merge rules, exactly as specified** — *"deletes already killed fallen and already existing fallen and merges them to your save"*: dedupe by `installId:ts` (records are immutable, so union IS the operation — no conflict resolution anywhere), drop what this world already rested, never re-import our own dead, cap at 40 with oldest-first eviction. Rest is PER WORLD: another player killing a corpse does not clear yours, which is what keeps five people's dead heavy. Rest records are their own append-only type keyed by corpse AND killer — that is what keeps the ledger a pure union, and it IS the separate roll. **THE LINEAGE NAME** — owner: *"how about keeping it medieval so the fallen is 'Francis child of Sasmooch'"*. `fallenTitle()` gives a travelled corpse its house; your own dead keep their bare name. ⚠ Deliberately the ROLL and PROSE name, NOT the combat name — the enemy stays `Hollowed Francis`, because every swing/block/miss line prints that name and a lineage in all of them reads as clutter by round three. The house lands where it has weight: the Arbiter naming what stood up, the roll, and the trophy line (`restRollLine`). ⚠⚠ **THE LORE — THE HOLLOWED ARE WARRIOR-CLASS AETHERKIN.** Owner: *"these fallen are a form of warrior class aetherkin that need lore. they are not able to rest because the reason you went down was too strong to let go of. your task still lingers on their lips and minds."* It slots into canon by INVERSION: the codex already has Aetherkin "trapped in an ethereal peace" — stone closed over them, work stopped, they slept. The Hollowed are the ones no stone ever closed over, because Aetherstone cannot encase a purpose still burning. They were never received; they simply stood back up, carrying the last errand worn down to a groove. ⚠ **AND IT EXPLAINS A RULE THAT WAS ONLY EVER MECHANICAL:** the faiths revere the SLEEPERS, the kin the Aether took in — a Hollowed is the opposite, so doctrine owes it release and nothing else. That is WHY no faction counts the kill against you and why the Arbiter calls it a mercy, not a hunt. The exemption keys on the TRAIT (ota991 proves a spoofed name can't break it), so the lore could name them Aetherkin outright without touching a line of wiring. New codex entry `hollowed` + a cross-reference appended to the base `aetherkin` entry, and the fight's own prose rewritten to carry it: the flavor says no stone ever closed over this one, the identity beat has the task moving on its lips "like a man counting steps in the dark", and the close finally gives them what they never had — *"They rest now — received at last."* Suite ota1362FallenLedger (29), including the hostile record walked end to end through the existing `reconstructFallenPiece` to prove it comes out inert. ⚠⚠ **AND THE WHOLE WIRING, on the owner's call to finish it.** New `fallenLedgerStore.ts`: a per-install identity minted once (names collide, installs don't), the HOUSE name that rides out with every corpse, the ledger on disk (re-validated on the way OFF disk too — an old build's file or a rooted phone's edit is as untrusted as a stranger's payload), and the exchange itself. **The join is one function.** `revenantPool()` = local un-avenged + imported un-rested; everything downstream — scaling, intro beats, the reclaim, the defeat lines — already worked on a FallenHero and never asks where it came from, so a foreign corpse is just one more name on the roll. **The defeat path branches on provenance:** a local corpse is marked avenged in place as always; a foreign one CANNOT be mutated (another house's ledger is not ours to edit, and mutation would break the union that lets two phones merge without a referee), so it gets an append-only rest record — which is both the separate roll and the receipt that travels home carrying how it died. ⚠ **THE DIFFICULTY DIAL, answered honestly:** pooling five players' dead against the flat 4% roll would have given VARIETY, not danger — each tile dices once, so the rate never moved. `revenantSpawnChance()` now climbs +1 point per un-rested foreign corpse, ceiling 12%. Deliberately gentle: raising it later is one number and no argument; shipping it too hot and taking it back costs a player their character. **Codex FALLEN tab** grows THE HOLLOWED (foreign dead, lineage-named, 'NEVER RECEIVED — the task is still on their lips'), PUT TO REST (the trophy roll), and THE EXCHANGE (house name + SEND MY DEAD / TAKE IN THEIRS via clipboard). Transport stays deliberately dumb — a string out, a string in — so the automatic mailbox lands as a caller of the same two functions and nothing above that seam learns the bytes started arriving on their own. Second suite ota1362LedgerWiring (12), centred on a REAL TWO-PHONE ROUND TRIP: install A exports its dead → B imports → the corpse rises in B's world titled *Francis child of Sasmooch* and builds a live boss → B puts it down → the receipt travels home and A reads how its own child ended. Plus: a looped-back payload never resurrects your own dead in your own wastes. ⚠ **AND AN EXISTING LOCK EARNED ITS KEEP:** ota1004 has always asserted that BOTH doors into the Hollowed — the wild spawner and the rumour route (`spawn_fallen_revenant`) — draw from one pool. The first cut of this wiring joined the wild spawner to the imported dead and left the rumour route on local dead only, so a tale could name a corpse from another house and always report a cold trail. The full suite caught it; both doors now call `revenantPool()`, the named lookup reads the joined pool too, and the lock now asserts on that call instead of the raw filter it replaced. ⚠⚠ **AND THE HANDSHAKE — SEND REQUEST / ACCEPT REQUEST.** Owner, on reviewing the first cut: *"where is the send request button, where is the accept button"*. Fair: the exchange shipped without one, which meant ANY well-formed payload imported. The validator makes a stranger's payload SAFE; it never made it WANTED, and those are different questions — before this, "who am I playing with" was answered by whoever pasted. **House cards:** `TAR1.<house|installId>.<checksum>` — one line to text, checksummed so a paste that lost characters in a chat window is REFUSED rather than silently pairing you to a broken id, and tolerant of arriving inside a sentence. **SEND REQUEST** copies yours; **ACCEPT REQUEST** takes theirs (from the field or the clipboard) and that store IS the authorization decision. Self-pairing is refused with an honest message; accepting twice is idempotent. **The gate:** unpaired dead are turned away at import — after the validator, so a hostile stranger is both sanitised AND refused, never one or the other — and reported separately (`unpaired`) from malformed ones (`rejected`). **CUT OFF** revokes a house: nothing further of theirs is admitted, but their Hollowed already standing in your wastes STAY, because you agreed to those and a corpse mid-errand does not evaporate when two houses fall out. Your own dead now meet TWO refusals — the gate first (you do not ride with yourself), the merge's own-dead rule underneath, proven by forcing the gate open in test. |
| 4.29.256 -> 4.29.257 | 2026-08-19-1363 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE LAST THREE PIECES: THE SEAL, THE MAILBOX, AND THE GEAR FAUCET.** Owner: *"build the three things not built yet."* **(1) THE SEAL — `fallenSeal.ts`.** Once the dead arrive from a shared box instead of a text, "this claims to be from a house you ride with" is worth nothing on its own: anyone who can write to the box can claim it. HMAC-SHA256 in plain TS (no native module, no dependency, so it still ships as an OTA), keyed on a sending key minted per install and handed out INSIDE the house card — which is now `TAR2`, with `TAR1` still parsing so a pre-seal pairing keeps working. The seal covers the body STRING and is checked BEFORE parsing, so tampered bytes never reach the parser. ⚠ Its honest limit, stated in the file: the key is symmetric and travels in the card, so whoever reads the card can forge that house — the same trust as the pairing itself, and the right level for a handful of friends. It is the THIRD of three separate questions, never a replacement for the other two: *sane?* (validator) · *wanted?* (pairing) · *theirs?* (seal). ⚠ **A hand-rolled hash that is subtly wrong is worse than none** — it looks like a signature and protects nothing — so the suite tests SHA-256 against the FIPS 180-4 digests, the 55/56/63/64-byte padding boundaries, HMAC against RFC 4231, and node's own crypto. Not the seal against itself. ⚠⚠ **AND ONE REAL BUG THE SUITES CAUGHT:** treating "I cannot verify this" as "this is a forgery". Senders always seal now, so a house paired before seals existed — no key on file — had every honest payload refused. With no key held there is nothing to check against, so it falls through to the pairing gate instead. Downgrades are still blocked: a house that DID give a key cannot later send unsealed. **(2) THE MAILBOX — `fallenMailbox.ts`.** Automatic delivery, shaped after `updates/apkRelease.ts` which has been doing exactly this for a year: one fetch, rate-limited, every failure swallowed whole so it can never reach the game thread. A GET to read a house's box, a PUT to leave yours — it is not a game server and holds no rules, so a private repo, a static host or a thirty-line worker all fit and swapping is a settings change. ⚠ **OFF until an address is typed in**: no default endpoint, no phone-home, no traffic on a build that never opted in. Auto-sync keeps a 15-minute floor and a rest nudges it, since a rest is the news other houses actually want. ⚠ **The mailbox gets NO shortcuts** — what it fetches goes through the same door a pasted payload does (seal → pairing gate → validator → merge), locked by a test that points it at an unpaired house and confirms the fetch is still refused. **(3) THE GEAR FAUCET — decided, not left open.** A foreign corpse yields its WEAPON only; armour rolls are skipped. The weapon is the emotional point (you carry their blade) and you can swing exactly one; armour is where the volume lives — four slots per corpse times five houses, arriving from saves this player does not control. ⚠ **Rarity is untouched on purpose:** degrading a foreign Legendary would lie about what that specific character carried. Cut the volume, keep the truth. Your own dead are unchanged. Suites ota1362Seal (10) and ota1362Mailbox (15). |

| 4.29.260 -> 4.29.261 | 2026-08-19-1367 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠ **TEN SLOTS, NOT SIX — the last cap OTA-1366 missed, plus a test file git could not read.** **(1) THE THIRD CAP.** OTA-1366 raised the kit cap from six to ten in TWO of the three places that truncate it — the death capture and `buildFallenGearSnapshot` — and left `revenantGearNames` at `slice(0, 6)`. | 4.29.264 -> 4.29.265 | 2026-08-19-1371 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE MINI-MAP MARKER SITS ON THE ROOM, EVEN WHERE THE VIEW CLAMPS.** Owner, comparing the two screens: *"the mini map doesn't quite line up — when you look at it on the regular map you're centered on the room; when you look in the mini map you're not centered under the room all the time."* Right, and it was mine. **THE CLAMP IS CORRECT; THE DOT WAS NOT.** The viewport deliberately stops sliding at the edges of the art so a rim room — the Gate, the Chapel, the Landings — does not drag empty space into frame. | 4.29.265 -> 4.29.266 | 2026-08-19-1372 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE ATLAS PINCH HOLDS WHAT IS BETWEEN YOUR FINGERS, AND THE MINI-MAP TILE SHARPENS.** **(1) THE PINCH.** Owner: *"when I use the two-finger gesture on the Atlas map to zoom in it doesn't stay centered on whatever was in the center of the screen — if I'm centered on the player, as I spread my fingers the player starts shooting down and to the left and I lose them off screen and have to find them again."* Two faults, compounding. **(a) NO FOCAL ANCHOR.** The handler raised the scale and left the translate to the raw pan delta. The scaled layer transforms about the BOX CENTRE — `screen = C + t + s·(p − C)` — so with `t` unchanged every content point flies away from C at `(s₁ − s₀)·(p − C)`: anything off the centre pixel drifts, and it drifts FASTER the further out it already was, which is exactly *"shoots off and I lose them"*. Centring on the player first does not save you, because ⌖ ME leaves him at the centre of the BOX while the pinch midpoint is wherever the hands are. Now solved properly — `t₁ = F₁ − K − (s₁/s₀)·(F₀ − K − t₀)`, K being the box centre in page coordinates — so the content under the fingers stays under the fingers. Pan falls out of the same expression (F₁ moving IS the pan), so the pinch branch no longer reads `gestureState` at all. **(b) THE PAN DELTA WAS DOUBLE-COUNTED.** `gestureState.dx` accumulates from the FIRST touch of the whole gesture and is never rebased. Both places that re-capture `startTx` — the second finger landing mid-gesture, and dropping from two fingers back to one — were folding the travel-so-far into the baseline and then adding it a second time out of `dx`, so every touch-count change jumped the map. All three capture points now rebase `dx` with the rest. **(2) THE TILE, 512 → 768.** Owner: *"it's a little grainy, we might need to bump the resolution up a little bit."* Correct, and the arithmetic says by how much: a ~130pt box drawn at 2.5× is ~325pt, which on a 2.4375× density screen is ~792 PHYSICAL pixels — 512 was being stretched half again past its own resolution, which is the grain. 768 lands at native and stops there: ~2.25MB decoded, still under half the ~6.0MB the real art would cost, and the next step buys nothing a phone can resolve. Suite ota1370MiniMap 19 → 25. |
| 4.29.266 -> 4.29.267 | 2026-08-19-1373 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE PINCH ANCHOR NOW KNOWS WHERE THE BOX ACTUALLY IS — the formula was right, the number fed to it was not.** Owner, after OTA-1372: *"zoom still slides away and doesn't stay centered on whatever the center of the screen was."* **THE FORMULA WAS CORRECT AND THE ORIGIN WAS WRONG.** Anchoring a pinch needs K, the box's PAGE origin, because touches arrive in page coordinates. OTA-1372 took it from an async `measureInWindow` fired inside `onLayout` — which lands late, may never fire, and goes stale the moment anything above the map changes height. Work the error through and it explains the symptom exactly: with K off by ε, `t₁' = t₁ + ε·(grow − 1)`. **That is ZERO at rest and grows with the zoom** — the map looks perfect until you start spreading, then it walks, and walks worse the harder you pinch. A right formula fed a wrong constant is indistinguishable from a wrong formula, which is why the first fix looked like it had not landed. **THE ORIGIN NOW COMES FROM THE TOUCH ITSELF.** Every touch carries `pageX` (screen space) and `locationX` (relative to the view it hit), so their difference IS that view's page origin — exact, synchronous, no callback to miss, and re-derived on every gesture so it cannot go stale. The measured value stays only as a fallback for any platform that does not report `locationX`. ⚠ **AND THE MAP LAYER IS NOW `pointerEvents="none"`, WHICH IS LOAD-BEARING.** `pageX − locationX` gives the origin of whatever view the touch TARGETED; with the transformed map layer able to take touches that would be its own moving origin and the anchor would be nonsense. Making it transparent puts the box itself under every touch. Nothing inside is tappable — zero `onPress` in the subtree, verified and now asserted, so adding one trips the test rather than quietly breaking the pinch. Suite ota1370MiniMap 25 → 29. |
| 4.29.267 -> 4.29.268 | 2026-08-19-1374 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE ATLAS ZOOMS ABOUT THE CENTRE OF THE SCREEN — the anchor the owner actually asked for, and it deletes the machinery the other one needed.** Owner, spelling out the gestures: *"One finger should drag from the point of contact in the direction of drag. 2 fingers moving apart should zoom in on what the center of the screen was, together should zoom [out]."* ⚠ **I HAD BUILT THE WRONG ANCHOR.** OTA-1372/1373 pinned the zoom to the point between the FINGERS — the photo-viewer convention, and a defensible reading of *"stay centered on whatever was in the center"*, but not what he asked for. Four pieces of coordinate plumbing, every one of them a place to be wrong — and OTA-1373 exists because one of them was. **ANCHOR ON THE CENTRE AND THE QUESTION DISAPPEARS.** The layer already transforms about its own centre — `screen = C + t + s·(p − C)` — so the content point at the centre is `(p* − C) = −t₀/s₀`, and holding it there through the zoom gives **`t₁ = (s₁/s₀)·t₀`**. One multiply. No page coordinates, no measurement, nothing to go stale, and it cannot drift because there is no origin left to get wrong. All four pieces of plumbing are deleted, and a test asserts they stay deleted — dead coordinate machinery around a gesture handler is how the next drift gets introduced. ⚠ **THE PINCH NO LONGER PANS,** deliberately: the centre is the fixed point, so sliding the pair around must not drag the map underneath it. Two fingers set the scale and nothing else; panning is the one-finger gesture and tracks 1:1 from the point of contact. **KEPT FROM OTA-1372:** `gestureState.dx` accumulates from the first touch of the whole gesture and is never rebased, so both places that re-capture the pan baseline were adding the travel-so-far twice and jumping the map on every change in touch count. That fix earned its keep and stays. Suite ota1370MiniMap (25). |

| 4.29.270 -> 4.29.271 | 2026-08-20-1377 | ⚠ GOLEM-ONLY TRIAL | ⚠⚠ **THE FREEZE BREADCRUMB CAN NOW TELL A CLEAN EXIT FROM A DEATH — a fix that SUBTRACTS.** Owner, before approving: *"so if I say close it are you putting in more trackers or a fix"* — a fix, and one that adds no instrument. No new key, no new write, no new log line, no new counter. **THE INVARIANT WAS NEVER ENFORCED.** The whole breadcrumb rests on one sentence in its own doc comment: *"a breadcrumb that SURVIVES to the next boot means the process died while it was still live."* That required something to clear it on the way out, and nothing did — `clearLiveBreadcrumb` had exactly ONE caller, `hydrate()`, which runs at BOOT. Worse than an omission: the OTA-1357 stamp at the top of the AppState handler WRITES a fresh crumb on the way to background and then leaves it there, so every cold start found one and the boot line reported a mid-action death whether or not anything had gone wrong. **An instrument that says the same thing every time carries no information** — and this is the instrument being used to hunt B9. The report block above it has been announcing `LAST BOOT DIED MID-ACTION` on sessions the owner closed cleanly. **THE FIX IS ONE CALL, PLACED CAREFULLY.** The crumb is cleared when the app reaches `background` — as the **LAST statement of the handler**, on purpose. OTA-1357 added its stamp at the TOP because the third B9 freeze died within 1ms of a state change, on a path nothing else covered; clearing at the top would have traded a false positive for a blind spot over exactly that window. Anything that dies earlier in the transition never reaches the clear, so the crumb survives and still names the transition it died in — and reaching the line is itself the proof the transition completed. ⚠ `background` ONLY, never `inactive`: iOS reports `inactive` for a notification banner, a Control Center pull, a peek at the app switcher, none of which is an exit. ⚠ **WHAT IT DOES NOT FIX, STATED PLAINLY:** background work that stamps a phase after this point re-arms the crumb, so an OS reclaim of a long-backgrounded app can still surface as a mid-action death. That is a far rarer shape than the one removed here — which fired on literally every clean exit — and closing it would mean recording a separate "exited cleanly" fact rather than deleting a stale one, i.e. the tracker the owner was right to ask about. Suite ota1377OrderlyExit (5). |
| 4.29.92 -> 4.29.93 | 2026-08-07-1160 | OTA | ⚠ REGEN WAS INVISIBLE ON EVERY SURFACE (twin of HAL 1183). Owner, with a screenshot of his own inventory: *"how am I supposed to know I had regen, I almost sold these. this is how we see them."* He was wearing **Echoing Steps Boots — `hpRegen: 2`, which is the ENTIRE `HP_REGEN_CAP`**, the most HP regen the game will grant from any number of pieces — and the row read `AC +2 · DEX +2`. Nothing anywhere said the boots were healing him every action; he nearly sold them, then asked why his health kept refilling. **⚠ Not one item: 93 of 293 armour pieces carry regen** (31 `hpRegen`, 62 `staminaRegen`). `previewArmor` built AC / Resists / statBonus / Durability and stopped — there was **no regen branch at all**, so not one of the 93 ever said so, on any surface: not the inventory row, not the item card, not the vendor list. A property the player cannot see is one they sell by accident, and on several Commons it is the best line on the item. The line names the **cadence** — `Regen: +2 HP per action` — because that is exactly what was misjudged: it ticks once per command in `submitPlayerAction`, not per hour and not per rest, and a bare "+2" reads as something slower. **⚠ Fused pieces show it too, and that is not incidental:** `aggregateEquippedRegen` resolves the worn piece by NAME via `findArmorByName` and never consults `uniqueStats`, so a fused copy keeps paying out — while the fused preview branch builds its lines from the ROLL and would have dropped the only mention of it. That branch now reads `ARMOR` by name, the same key the payout uses. The rolled-instance path needed nothing: it rebuilds by keeping every line that is not AC / stat / durability, so placing the regen line before Durability carries it for free. New suite ota1160RegenIsVisible (8 tests), which walks the **whole catalogue** rather than spot-checking the reported item — the defect was a missing branch, so one item proves nothing. **⚠ Two corrections to the record, both from this session's device-log reads:** (1) *"the player has never defeated an enemy"* was **wrong** — the `defeated=0` in the persist line is `saveTrim`'s **kilobyte** measurement of `worldMemory.defeatedEnemies`, not a count; 28 short names round to 0 KB, and his sheet reads 28 defeated. Read `saveSizeBreakdown` before quoting any of its numbers. (2) There is **no HP-milestone bug** — starting HP is `rollDice(5, 10)` + race bonus, so 29 at day 26 is an ordinary roll. |
| 4.29.91 -> 4.29.92 | 2026-08-07-1159 | OTA | ⚠ THE LAST TWO DESIGN CALLS (twin of HAL 1182) — the third and fourth of the four OTA-1156 held, decided together. **⚠ (3) THE SCALER NOW KNOWS WHAT YOU ARE WEARING AND SWINGING.** Owner: his AC went 20 → 26 and the difficulty did not move. It could not — `enemyScalePower` was `bestCombatStat + hpMax/10`, and AC was not an input at all, nor was weapon damage. Meanwhile `powerRating.playerPowerScore`, the number on the player's own sheet, is `bestStat + damage + AC + hp/10`: **two answers to "how strong is this character", and the one the player SAW counted their armour while the one that SET THE DIFFICULTY did not.** ⚠ The new terms are SCALED, not added — `overLevelT` maps power 14 → 32 and raw AC is 10-26, the same magnitude as the entire old formula, so adding it whole pins every armoured character at max difficulty forever. Both are measured **above a fresh-arrival baseline** and divided into a 0-4 band, deliberately the same width as the HP term, because AC and HP are the two survivability axes and neither should drown the other. **⚠ A fresh arrival is unchanged to the decimal, by construction:** at the baselines both terms are zero or negative and they clamp at zero, so gear can never make the world *easier* than authored. Measured — fresh `0.000 → 0.000`, owner at AC 20 `0.444 → 0.542`, owner at AC 26 `0.444 → 0.583`, end-game fused `0.722 → 0.917`; nothing saturates. Ships at **half weight** (`GEAR_POWER_BLEND = 0.5`) so the curve moves once, visibly, and can be read off a device log before we commit to the rest; full weight is that one token and no other edit. **Seven spawners each hand-rolled the formula** — which is exactly why AC stayed missing, there was nowhere to add it once — and all now route through `scalePowerOf`, taking AC from `standingAc` rather than a third re-derivation of a number OTA-1133 already exists to settle. **⚠ Latent bug found and guarded:** `getEquippedWeapon` does `for (const it of player.inventory)` with no guard and throws on an inventory-less player; several spawn paths call it inside a `try/catch` that swallows, so the throw would not surface as an error — it would surface as **an encounter that silently never happens**. It falls back to the baselines (gear term exactly 0, i.e. the pre-OTA number): if we cannot see the gear we scale as though there is none rather than inventing difficulty from a guess. **⚠ (4) THE CONTRACT REFUSAL STOPS BLAMING TRAVEL.** The empty-list line was *"Nothing for you right now — check back after I've travelled."* **There is no restock** — `availableFactionQuests` filters a static authored pool by rep and by what the player already took, so travelling changes nothing, ever. It promised a mechanic that does not exist and sent the player away to do the one thing that provably cannot help: the OTA-1158 defect class, in the one place it costs the player *time* rather than only misinforming them. An empty list has exactly two causes needing opposite actions, so it now says which — LOCKED names the count and the **cheapest** rung still out of reach (not the highest; telling someone two points off a rep-8 contract that they need 25 is the same unhelpfulness in a new costume), CLEARED says to try another banner. Measured: **38 of 65 faction quests are rep-gated, 5 → 25, with eight at rep 25 — above the join threshold of 20** — but every faction offers exactly 2 at rep 0, so no fresh player ever meets an empty board. **⚠ All four held calls are now discharged, and each hold was INVERTED rather than deleted**, so every decision is as hard to undo as the hold was. **⚠ (5) THE HOSTILE SPILLOVER IS METERED ON THE GAIN SIDE.** Owner: *"just nerf it a bit like you suggested"*, superseding an earlier *"leave it"* on the same item. Every standing LOSS cascades — allies take half, **rivals take the inverse and GAIN**: a caught theft is −10 / −5 / **+5 to every rival**, an extortion −6 / −3 / **+3**. Gifts have carried a lifetime per-faction budget since OTA-803 and this path had **nothing**, so shaking down a faction's enemies was an unbounded climb with them — Conspiracy Architects have four rivals and start at −20, and ~14 extortions of their enemies reached the join threshold, repeatable forever. Now metered against `SPITE_STANDING_FACTION_CAP` (10 lifetime per faction): **20 shakedowns pay +10 once instead of +60.** ⚠ **Only the gains.** Being *hated* stays uncapped — a capped consequence is one a player can spend past — and the raw −10 / −6 magnitudes are untouched; this caps spillover, not punishment. Same asymmetry, same reason, as the gift budget it mirrors. ⚠ The excess is **rolled back off the standing rows**, not merely omitted from the log: a gain that moved the number while going unreported would be the OTA-1156 defect (a log disagreeing with the save) pointing the other way. New suites ota1159ScalerKnowsGear (12 tests), ota1159RefusalTellsTruth (8 tests) and ota1159SpiteMeter (8 tests). ⚠ Heavy sims NOT claimed green: `combatStress` and `statGrowthBalanceSim` pass with this change; `encounterStress`'s skirmish-spawn test fails and fails **identically on clean HEAD** — it is the known-red suite already documented in §5. |

From the device log on OTA-1166: `craft Frost Paste` → `resolved=Searing Paste`, and a second instance the same session, `craft Blue Cap Draught` → `resolved=mountain capital`. **The symptom looked cosmetic and was not.** Both crafts produced the right item — craft matches RECIPES, not inventory — so the only visible damage was a wrong word in a debug line; but ~25 gameStore handlers read `parsed.resolvedNoun` BEFORE `parsed.target`, so the same resolution under `use` spends the wrong consumable and the player never learns why. Mechanism, `engine/parser.ts` resolveItem: pass 2 matched on the item's HEAD NOUN (last word of the name) and never consulted the rest — "frost paste" → head "paste" → the first inventory row ending in "paste" wins, "frost" discarded. **This is OTA-093's bug seen from the other side:** that OTA closed the mirror image (adjective-ONLY input like "bone" → Bone Fragment no longer wins) and its comment says so in as many words; nobody checked the direction where the INPUT is the side carrying the adjective — same function, same comment block, thirteen lines apart. Fix, in order: (a) a candidate whose other name words all appear in the input wins outright, which also settles "mud-rend blade" vs "Rusted Blade" without the off-hand hint; (b) nothing agrees and the input carried no extra token, so nothing is being ignored — keep the historical first-match ("use the blade"); (c) nothing agrees and the input DID carry a token no candidate accounts for — return undefined, because a miss is recoverable and a confident wrong answer spends an item. **The guard had to cover pass 3 too** or it does nothing: pass 3 fuzzy-matches tokens against head nouns, and fuzzyEqual('paste','paste') hands Searing Paste straight back one loop later — both passes now route through one picker. Pass 1 (full-name containment) untouched. New suite ota1149TheModifierIsEvidence (8 tests); all parser suites re-run green, resolveItem being load-bearing for the whole verb table. |
| 4.29.80 -> 4.29.81 | 2026-08-06-1148 | OTA | ⚠ THE FIRST WORD SURVIVES (twin of HAL 1171; owner: "the arbiter has started speaking and has either skipped his first word or started partway through it"). **This is OTA-790's bug at the other end of the buffer.** That OTA widened the TAIL guard 8 → 40 ms because "a fading fricative sits well under the 0.01 threshold" and was being trimmed to within 8 ms of the last loud sample; an ONSET is the same physics in reverse, and the head guard was still 8 ms — four lines away in the same function. trimSilenceLeadTrail scans in to the first sample over 0.01 and hands back a guard pad, so every quiet onset was walked straight past: "Welcome" opens on a /w/ glide ramping from near zero, "The" on a weak voiced /ð/, and /h/ /s/ /f/ are breath before they are sound. With 8 ms returned the word began MID-VOWEL, or the consonant vanished outright and it sounded like a skipped word. **Head guard 8 ms → 45 ms**, wider than the tail's 40 because onsets run longer than decays (aspiration and frication precede voicing). The 0.01 threshold and the 200 ms/end trim cap are untouched — a long silent lead is still trimmed; this only widens what is KEPT in front of the onset. New suite ota1148TheFirstWordSurvives (8 tests), which re-implements the trimmer's arithmetic and pins its constants against the source so the two cannot drift apart silently. |

The earlier OTA that closed this as working-as-designed is RE-AUTHORED rather than re-numbered, because its claim changed. **2. It is a prefix rule now, the bigger half:** five entries were enumerated and the content carries TWENTY — Aetherkin, Aethercraft(ed/ers), Aetherium, Aetherforge(d), Aetherstorm(s), Aetherwing, Aetherwave, Aetherflame, Aetherlight, Aetherbound, Aethereal, Aetherons all fell through to espeak's raw letter-to-sound rules. A prefix rule cannot miss the next word someone authors. Named compounds keep their space before a real word (stone/born/bat/kin); bare suffixes (-ic, -ium) stay attached so stress lands ay-THER-ik. **⚠ The sort needed a second key:** the catch-all's source is LONGER than the named compound it must never pre-empt, so length-descending alone would have silently collapsed "ayther stone" into "aytherstone". Catch-alls (patterns not ending in \b) now sort after every named entry; the catch-all also lowercases the tail so an all-caps run can't reach espeak as letters. | 4.29.78 -> 4.29.79 | 2026-08-06-1146 | OTA | ⚠ "THE" IS TWO WORDS (twin of HAL 1169; owner: "kokoro pronounces the as thee it should be pronounce thuh or tha"). English has two articles spelled "the": /ðə/ ("thuh") before a consonant SOUND, /ðiː/ ("thee") before a vowel sound — speakers switch without noticing, which is why the wrong one grates. **The sound decides, not the letter**, and a bare /^[aeiou]/ test gets the two commonest shapes in this game's prose backwards: "the hour" is THEE (silent h), "the university" is THUH (u says "yoo"), "the unknown" is THEE (u says "uh"). Two whole-word ANCHORED exception lists carry it, so a `uni` prefix can't swallow "uninformed"/"uninvited"/"unimportant". **It runs LAST, after the respellings** — load-bearing, because the lexicon turns "Aether" into "ay thur" (consonant-initial on the page, vowel-initial in the mouth), so the rule judges the text espeak actually receives. **One knob:** THE_SCHWA_RESPELLING = 'thuh'; the real risk is voicing (espeak-ng gives word-initial `th` its voiceless reading for unknown words), alternates "thuh" → "thu" → "tha". Needs an ear on device — jest mocks Kokoro. TTS-only; the visible log keeps "the". New suite ota1146TheIsTwoWords (12 tests); loreLexicon's two article-bearing fixtures retargeted. |

Fixed where it belongs: it only fires while the player is on a STATIONARY screen (`uiIdleSince`, the owner's own homework signal, which is null during a load), and it runs as HOMEWORK — below voice, and cut short when real work arrives. **The reservation shrinks 1200 → 350 ms**: with the cause gone it is a guard rail sized to the real handoff, not a mechanism anything relies on. The fire-and-forget pin retargeted to the homework call. New suite ota1145NobodyWasWaitingOnIt (8 tests). |
| 4.29.76 -> 4.29.77 | 2026-08-06-1144 | OTA | ⚠ THE HANDOFF WINDOW (twin of HAL 1167; owner, on the next load: "reintroduced llm lag"). Device log: greeting on screen at :25.994, `item_synthesis preempted 3565ms in 328t→out 0t` at :29.722, `voice⏱ gap 4720ms (wait 3604ms + synth 849ms)` at :30.714. **Not the welcome-back OTA** — that load was ~90 min after the last, so neither the ≥6h while-away beat nor the ≥4h offline recap armed; the single greeting is exactly what it promises, and it touches no LLM or TTS code. **Two holes, and the interruptible-synthesis OTA closed neither for this job shape.** (1) The job started AFTER the line was queued: speak() stamps queuedAt and hands to drain(), which awaits the voice model and a durable crash breadcrumb before it ever calls runExclusiveNativeMl — the synthesis took the lock inside that gap, and priority cannot order work that has not arrived. (2) `out 0t` — the preempt could not land: stopCompletion() is polled in llama.cpp's DECODE loop and this job never reached decode, so all 3565 ms was PREFILL of a 328-token prompt (~11 ms/tok); it saved ~40 ms of a ~3.6 s wait. Interruption only ever covered the decode half. **Fix: stop the job STARTING.** A voice line that needs synthesis reserves the native-ML lock the moment it is accepted for speech; work below voice defers until it arrives or the deadline passes (VOICE_RESERVATION_MS 1200). Bounded by construction — deadline, explicit release when the audio is in hand or the queue drains, and no reservation at all for a BANKED line (which plays from memory and needs no lock, so reserving would make the pre-synthesis bank cost narration the time it exists to save). Exclusivity untouched: this schedules starts, never overlaps ops — pinned by test. SO HOMEWORK GETS TWO THINGS, and they only work together: (1) ML_PRIORITY_HOMEWORK, BELOW voice, so it never jumps a queue; and (2) an onPreempt hook — the moment higher-priority work is ENQUEUED (not pumped; by pump time the player has already waited out the whole job), llama.cpp is told to stop, the promise settles with whatever it had, the lock frees, and the player's call runs. Partial homework is discarded: it was free work, losing it costs nothing, and making the player wait costs the only thing that matters. ⚠ EXCLUSIVITY IS UNTOUCHED and outranks every latency concern here: preemption does not overlap two native ops, it asks the running one to FINISH EARLY, and the chain still waits for it to settle before pumping. The arb159 crash guarantee is exactly as strong, and the suite asserts max-concurrency is 1 through a preemption with a lingering native call. ⚠ ORDINARY WORK IS NEVER PREEMPTED — narration and voice supply no hook, so there is nothing to fire; finishing them IS the point. New telemetry outcome 'preempted', deliberately NOT filed with the failures (yielding to the player is the feature working) with its own ⏸ mark so a session full of them still shows up without reading as a fault. ⚠ AND IT SHIPS WITH A LIVE CONSUMER rather than as scaffolding: the bank's rest-filler is by definition work nobody asked for, so it becomes the first homework job ('ambient_fill'), priced separately from the live 'ambient' line which is NOT homework because the player is owed it. New suite ota1123HomeworkPreempt (15 tests); four assertions retargeted. |
| 4.29.55 -> 4.29.56 | 2026-08-05-1122 | OTA | ⚠ THE BANK (twin of HAL 1145). Step two of the LLM headroom track. The economics of an ambient musing were upside down: it is generated ON DEMAND, takes 8-16 seconds, and is then checked against the world it was composed for. The OTA-1130 telemetry says two of three came back unusable — ~16.6 seconds of model time for lines nobody read — and the single biggest killer is `stale`: the player kept playing while the model wrote, so by the time the line existed it no longer belonged to the moment. ⚠ THAT IS A RACE WE WERE NEVER GOING TO WIN BY GENERATING FASTER. So stop racing. A musing is UNPROMPTED by construction (AMBIENT_INSTRUCTION forbids it from reacting to the last action), which is exactly the property that makes it pre-generatable: write them when the world is standing still, spend them when it isn't. TWO SOURCES OF STOCK. (1) REST — the player has explicitly chosen to pass time, the world is standing still by definition, and the shared native-ML lock is as free as it ever gets; fired AFTER the rest resolves so a slow generation never delays recovery, and BEFORE the ambush spawn so a fight leaves the bank untouched rather than poisoning it. (2) ⚠ STALE LINES, WHICH ARE NOT WASTED LINES — `stale` almost always means "you walked somewhere else", and the line is still perfectly good FOR THE PLACE IT WAS WRITTEN ABOUT; banked against its own stamp, walking back into that room spends it instantly instead of paying for it twice. The discard that was the headline waste becomes the stock. SPENDING IS FREE, so the withdraw sits ABOVE the model-readiness and cooldown gates (the bank works while Qwen is reloading, and a line that costs nothing is not rationed by a cooldown that exists to ration generations) and BELOW the combat and tutorial muzzles (which are about whether a musing is wanted at all). ⚠ VALIDITY IS ambientStaleReason UNCHANGED — the same five checks the live path already ran, asked at SPEND time instead of at finish time. That is the whole trick: a banked line cannot go stale between being wanted and being spoken, because there is no gap. Plus the near-duplicate check, which matters more here because a banked line may have sat through a dozen others. Bank is session-scoped, capped at 3, oldest-out, no duplicates; a banked line is DEFERRED work rather than WASTED work so it no longer counts against qwenWasteTotals — that number decides whether a job is worth keeping and must not indict the fix. New suite ota1122TheBank (16 tests); one OTA-1130 assertion retargeted. |
| 4.29.54 -> 4.29.55 | 2026-08-05-1121 | OTA | ⚠ THE CACHED PREFIX (twin of HAL 1144). First real win of the LLM headroom track, and it costs nothing — no new generation, no new model, no content removed. ⚠ OTA-1131 measured prefix reuse at exactly ZERO across a whole session and recorded it as "the cache saved us nothing". That reading was wrong in a useful way: llama.cpp reuses the longest COMMON PREFIX between the previous prompt and this one, and every prompt we built put a VARIABLE line SECOND (the room name), so the reusable prefix ended after ~14 tokens no matter how much byte-identical text followed. Measured on the ambient prompt: 327 tokens, of which 287 were identical on EVERY call, of which 19 were ever reused — about 2.8 seconds per ambient line at the measured ~10.5ms/token, spent re-reading text the model had already read. FIXED BY ORDER, NOT CONTENT: STABLE PREFIX -> VARIABLE BODY -> IMPERATIVE TAIL. Reusable prefix between consecutive calls, before -> after: ambient->ambient 19 -> 271 tokens; narration->combat 114 -> 456; narration->ambient 14 -> 98. About 2.6s off a repeat ambient line and 3.6s off a combat line. The prompt did NOT get smaller (356 -> 359 tokens on ambient) — this is pure reordering, and the suite asserts it so a later edit cannot "improve" the number by deleting content. ⚠ THE IMPERATIVE STAYS LAST: a 0.5B model follows the instruction nearest the generation point best, so the instruction blocks are SPLIT (rules in front where they cache, task at the back where it binds) rather than moved wholesale. ⚠ THE PREAMBLE IS SHARED ACROSS JOBS ON PURPOSE: the cache holds ONE sequence, so an ambient call after a combat call reuses only what those two prompts share; an identical opening across ambient/peaceful/combat means the preamble survives a job switch. Every sentence keeps its original wording — the only rewrites are the "above"/"below" pointers the move forces (OTA-1131 is the standing lesson that a prompt lying about its own layout costs a whole generation), and combat's "entities listed above" is deliberately NOT flipped because its task still sits after the entity block. New suite ota1121CachedPrefix (13 tests); two OTA-1131 assertions retargeted. |
| 4.29.53 -> 4.29.54 | 2026-08-05-1120 | OTA | ⚠ THE VOLLEY AFTER A KILL (twin of HAL 1143). That else reads as "the enemy is dead, there is nothing left to counter with" — true in the SOLO fight those paths were first written for, false the moment a pack is involved. A killing blow bought the player the ENTIRE group's round: kill one raider of five and the other four never swung; chain it one kill per round and a pack fight costs nothing at all. The four: the melee attack path, the Beacon Rifle bolt volley, the coating-throw burst (whose own comment already promised "the group still swings back"), and the dog's killing bite — which returned straight past the arb169 volley eighty lines below, so a dog that KILLED bought the free round a dog that merely BIT did not, which is the arb169 exploit wearing a kill for a hat. | 4.29.52 -> 4.29.53 | 2026-08-05-1119 | OTA | ⚠ THE DORMANCY WINDOW, AND THE WORD THAT HID IT (twin of HAL 1142). The owner's device log, three consecutive lines: "OTA session start" / "item_synthesis empty 8809ms read 0ms/write 0ms in 309t->out 0t (0ch)" / "qwen-watchdog: Qwen dormant - reinitializing". 8.8 seconds of wall time with ZERO prefill and ZERO decode — the model did not think slowly, it never ran. ⚠ ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS ORDERING: isDormant() is defined as "status==='ready' but the runtime is gone", and QwenGenerativeEngine.dispose() was PRODUCING that state for the length of its own teardown. LlamaRuntime.dispose() nulls its context SYNCHRONOUSLY on entry (the arb crash fix — detach before release so nothing starts a completion against a context about to be freed), then AWAITS ctx.release() behind the shared native-ML lock, waiting out whatever generation holds it; the engine only set status='idle' AFTER that await returned. From the first line of teardown until the lock cleared, the engine reported ready-over-dead — the watchdog's exact dormancy signature — for as long as the in-flight generation ran (10.4s in the owner's log). The OTA session start is also the one shutdown path that runs in the FOREGROUND, where OTA-1107's don't-reload-while-backgrounded guard does not apply. FIXED by moving one line: status leaves 'ready' first instead of last. Costs nothing, closes the window entirely; the OTA-1107 lifecycleGen bump still comes first of all. ⚠ SECOND HALF: `empty` was hiding two different failures — a model that genuinely produced nothing is a PROMPT problem, a call against a detached context is a LIFECYCLE problem, and the two get investigated in opposite directions. New telemetry outcome 'dormant', recorded on both the empty and the throw path by checking this.context AFTER the await, with its own rollup mark (∅ = the model said nothing, 💀 = there was no model to say it). ⚠ PLUS ONE UNRELATED OWNER REQUEST: the death screen holds 16s instead of 11s — "increase the delay on death before it goes to the character collection screen by 5 seconds. they can always tap to close if they want." The tap-to-leave escape is what makes a long hold safe: erring long costs an impatient player one tap, erring short costs a reading player their character's ending. New suite ota1119DormancyWindow (13 tests), whose first assertion drives a real teardown and checks isDormant() mid-flight — it fails against the old ordering. |

| 4.29.50 -> 4.29.51 | 2026-08-05-1117 | OTA | ⚠ THE RULE DIALS, AND ONE THAT WAS A CONTROL OVER NOTHING (twin of HAL 1140). Closes the difficulty set OTA-1136 opened. | 4.29.49 -> 4.29.50 | 2026-08-05-1116 | OTA | ⚠ THE ELITE SWAP (twin of HAL 1139). The last of the nine difficulty dials (OTA-1136) to find a consumer, and the one the industry survey rates highest: the CONTENT lever. On a hit, the party that would have crested the rise arrives as ONE named body instead. ⚠ WHY THIS LEVER AND NOT ANOTHER MULTIPLIER: a multiplier makes the same fight LONGER — this project has been bitten by that once, and it is why scaledPackSize is welded to scaledSwingCap. A content swap makes the fight DIFFERENT. Four raiders is a targeting and attrition problem; one Reaver-Captain is a puzzle (what is it weak to, do I have that coating, is my resist profile wrong). Those are different verbs — and the second set of systems (bestiary, weakness tags, coatings, resistances) is most valuable against ONE durable body and nearly irrelevant against four disposable ones. It is also structurally incapable of the stall tail: one body is one attacker, so the swing cap can never bind. It is the ANTI-pack, which is why a tier running both hot is more varied than either alone. ⚠ AND IT ADDS NO NEW BALANCE CONSTANTS, which is what makes the claim checkable. scaleEncounterForContext already encodes the rule: a PACK is anchored on one solo-equivalent plus 22% per extra body and gets only 0.6x of the attack/AC bump "because there are several of them"; a SOLO foe gets the full bump. So the elite is the pack's OWN summed HP concentrated into one body, hitting at the solo rate — the fold runs AFTER pack scaling (that total IS the budget), the single body is re-scaled through the solo branch, and the total is given back. Durability from the pack, aggression from the solo; retune the pack and the elite retunes free. ⚠ THE CARRY IS THE ONLY WAY THIS COULD HAVE UNDERPAID: spoils are rolled PER CORPSE, so a body replacing four would have paid one corpse's worth for a four-body fight — paid LESS for a harder encounter, the exact fake-difficulty trap. eliteReplaced buys n-1 extra loot rolls, on the ROLL COUNT rather than a flat grant, so spoils stay drawn from its own table. AUDIT FINDINGS, recorded in eliteSwap.ts: there are NO "kill N of X" objectives in the game (faction quests are fetch / escort / staged, and a staged kill advances ONE stage per kill, so a collapsed party costs encounters, never completions); the +1 max-HP milestone keys on DISTINCT enemy types not total kills (arb119 anti-farm), so collapsing identical grunts costs nothing and a NAMED elite is a new type; hunts match by EXACT NAME, so this must never touch a hunt or boss spawn. Bodies are named for that reason and two more — an unannounced stat-inflated grunt reads as a bug, and the announce lines stop claiming a headcount that is no longer true. New suite ota1116EliteSwap (29 tests). |

Of course it continued the pattern; the example told it to. ⚠ SO NO ALTERNATION EVER APPEARS INSIDE THE JSON. The example is now a CONCRETE, VALID OBJECT the model can copy verbatim and be right, and the allowed values live in prose beside it where a pipe cannot be mistaken for content. Same fields, same validator, about the same length — this costs nothing. ⚠ AND WHY NEITHER EARLIER FIX COULD HAVE WORKED, which is the part worth keeping: a model looping on | loops on | at ANY token budget and ANY prompt length. Both earlier reads were reasonable on the evidence then available, and only the raw-text instrumentation OTA-1132 added could tell this apart from a model that rambles — the instrumentation was the fix that mattered. The loop also gets its own discard name (item_synth:alternation-loop) so a recurrence names itself in one word. New suite ota1115PipeLoop; ota1132 and ota1131 retargeted off the pipe form they were asserting. |
| 4.29.47 -> 4.29.48 | 2026-08-05-1114 | OTA | ⚠ GROUP EQUIP / UNEQUIP, AND THE ONE BUTTON THAT WAS LEFT (twin of HAL 1137).
