The same session's log shows AC drop 16 → 10 mid-fight against five raiders, then a hit on nearly every incoming swing and death four rounds later — "I could have won that five-person fight just by standing there and punching them." ⚠ WHY A PLANNER AND NOT A LOOP OVER equipItem(): slots have CAPACITY. Two chest pieces cannot both go on, a two-hander eats both hands, rings have three slots. A for-loop would "succeed" every call and end with ONE piece worn and four quietly displaced, while the button promised five. A group action's count is a PROMISE, so planGroupEquip resolves contention BEFORE the button is drawn: first-in-list wins the slot, and anything crowded out is NAMED in the bar and again in the confirm. Capacity is seeded FULL rather than from what is worn, because equipping SWAPS exactly as the one-at-a-time path always has; the only thing refused is two SELECTED pieces racing each other, the one case the player could not have predicted. EQUIP and TAKE OFF render BEFORE scrap so the reversible action is under your thumb, and the take-off confirm says what it costs ("your armor class drops by what they were worth") because armor IS armor class and the log above is what it looks like when nobody says so. And it says it LOUDER mid-fight ("you are in a fight; every swing coming at you lands easier the moment this is off") because taking armor off in a quiet room is housekeeping and taking it off with five raiders on the tile is the last decision the character makes. UNEQUIP clears SLOTS not items — a two-hander holds main AND off, and clearing one would leave it half-wielded. New suite ota1114GroupEquip (23 tests). |

NINE DIALS, tagged by the survey's three lever types: MULTIPLIERS (spawn, discovery, pack — both directions, loot, hunger), RULE CHANGES (curios stay unidentified; no weakness tags the bestiary has not earned — the survey rates these the best value in the medium), CONTENT SWAP (elite: a group of grunts sometimes arrives as one dangerous body — "the good one"). Bug the tests caught: isPressureTier() tested PRESSURE_ORDER membership, which excludes 'custom', silently demoting every custom character to the default and making the picker a no-op. |
| 4.29.45 -> 4.29.46 | 2026-08-05-1112 | OTA | THE OUTDOOR HALF OF OTA-1032 (twin of HAL 1135). Owner: "fix the rest ambush line dedup, or should the llm rework be giving a new line for that?" | 4.29.44 -> 4.29.45 | 2026-08-05-1111 | OTA | HALF (twin of HAL 1134). Owner: "there were sections that every move or rest was a fight … let's just 1/2 the chance percentage." THE ARITHMETIC HE WAS FEELING: the wasteland encounter roll was 0.58 auto-travel / 0.45 manual × the time-of-day multiplier (1.3 night / 0.85 day) — a **75% chance PER STEP** auto-travelling after dark. And the gate meant to space encounters out does the opposite for the player who needs it most: `tileIsNovel` tests `recentTileHistory`, which starts EMPTY, so a brand-new character has every tile novel and rolls the full chance every step while an established player working known ground rolls far less often — the rate peaked exactly where the character was weakest. The owner spotted this from the description alone ("if we are looking for not traveled in the last 50 squares, doesn't that penalize new players since they haven't been anywhere?") and explained it from his play pattern ("I have been starting new characters and running them through the paces for 1 to 2 capitals"). ⚠ WHY A FLAT HALVING AND NOT THE EARLY-GAME RAMP proposed first: the ramp reached full rate at ~25 tiles and the owner rejected it on sight — "the 25 step block means they can make it to half of the capitals with no encounters." Right, and it generalises: the problem was never that new characters deserve a discount, it is that the BASELINE was too high for everybody and the novelty gate merely made the opening where you noticed. A ramp would have papered over a bad number with a second mechanic and bought a dead opening. 0.58 → 0.29, 0.45 → 0.225; night auto-travel 75% → ~38%, day manual 38% → ~19%; the auto-travel-is-more-eventful ratio preserved exactly by construction. UNTOUCHED on instruction or on measurement: late-game scaling ("they should be tied to a difficulty level choice in character creation" — engine/pressure.ts already exists and currently declines to scale encounter rate on purpose, so wiring it is a design job, logged in HANDOFF); REST ambush (22%/8% base → 18.7%/28.6% with the multiplier; the two-for-two log rest is a ~5% coincidence — the real fault is both drawing the IDENTICAL line, a dedup problem); and the novelty gate itself (still doing its anti-farm job). |

THE SCREEN: the opening crawl asks why you came down and offers five motives; nothing ever answered it (death was three log lines, a silent 3.5s hold, and a cut to the slot list). The ending is now written from the SAME motive — engine/deathScene.ts + data/story/death.json, three variants each — so an exile dies "in a place that never learned the name" and a scholar's account "ends mid-sentence". Three beats (the fall, the motive's answer, a plain ledger of days and kills) plus the Arbiter's last word. DeathOverlay is the intro overlay's sibling with three deliberate differences: a SLOW 1.6s fade (the intro drifts up because you are arriving; this only darkens), NO SKIP (a tap leaves early only once the text is legible, so a player mid-tap when they died cannot dismiss their own death unread), and an 11s self-handover so a phone set down mid-fight never returns to a stuck modal. Variant seeded from the death stamp — a re-render cannot reshuffle words being read. ⚠ THE LOCKDOWN, the half that was broken: (1) the parley intimidate-an-animal failure dealt 3+d6 with NO DEATH CHECK — every other damage site (DOTs, weather, falls, counters, effect damage) calls handlePlayerDeath at zero, this one did not, and the counter volley after it bails at hp<=0, leaving a living character on exactly 0 with no epitaph and no death. Now routed through `playerIsDownNotDead`, which reads LIVE state because the bug class is code acting on a player captured before the killing blow. (2) FOUR `Math.max(1, …)` HP floors on the equip/displace paths silently resurrected: the floor rightly stops an hpMax cut stranding a LIVING player on zero, but at ZERO it stood a corpse back up — equipping gear was a one-point revive. All four now route through `hpAfterMaxChange` (floor for the living, 0 for the dead). The handover also voids anything queued behind the death and is idempotent against a tap racing the timer. |

(2) THE DIMMING SAYS THE RIGHT WHY — a row the budget can no longer pay for is dimmed, un-tappable, and says the pieces already picked are spending its materials; a row that was never affordable is blocked too but keeps its existing "Missing:" line, because the other reason would be a lie. (3) The bar takes REPAIR ALL's slot above the ScrollView and carries the running bill. (4) The confirm NAMES any picked piece the budget can't pay for ("These stay damaged") — stock can shift under a group, and a group that mends six of seven without saying which it skipped is the bulk-action failure this run keeps being written against. | 4.29.0 -> 4.29.1 | 2026-08-03-1067 | OTA | PHASE 5 -- THE ARBITER BECOMES SOMEONE (twin of HAL 1090). Plan verbatim: "memory of what you've done, an opinion that shifts with your choices, and an arc across the nine Cores. STANCE (where he is): an arc across the nine Cores -- witness / interested / invested / implicated / named at 0/1/3/6/9 -- rising only, changing WHAT he will talk about. REGARD (what he thinks of you): a score summed from conduct the run already records, banded cold/wary/even/warm/kin, moving both ways, changing HOW he says it via three tones (not five -- five produced pairs nobody could tell apart in play). MEMORY (what he names): the person you wronged, the debt you squared, the Phase 3 question you answered, by NAME; every third remark reaches for one when there is one. ⚠ DERIVED, NOT STORED -- the Phase 3 rule again. Stance and regard are pure functions of the save; the ONLY new persisted field is player.arbiterBeatsSeen, the one-shot lines already SPOKEN, because "has he said this yet" genuinely is not derivable. Absent on an old save reads as "he has not said any of it", so a long-running character hears the beats it has earned rather than silently missing the arc. ⚠ AND NOTHING IN THE MODULE ROLLS DICE: he is one continuous voice, so his opinion must be a function of your conduct, not a coin -- callers pass a rotation counter and the same save always yields the same line. ⚠ THE OPINION CANNOT BE FARMED: every input is clamped and the total clamped again (lore +8, gifts +6, relics preserved +8, forks ±20, wrongs -24, corruption -15, menace -12), so what moves regard late is conduct that COSTS something; the negatives are clamped for the opposite reason, because cold must be a place a fifty-hour run can climb out of. All 29 Phase 3 fork options carry a signed regard value AND an echo phrase in his own words, with a test failing the build if either side has an orphan -- the OTA-1064 audit lesson applied across files. FIVE ROUTES IN, each tested for REACHABILITY rather than for existing: scene narration (the 15% personal-beat branch asks the persona first, old pool only when there is no character to read); arrival (one due beat, yielding to a tide crossing rather than stacking on it -- unspoken means unconsumed, so it lands next time instead of being lost); "what is your name", matched BEFORE every lookup that would swallow it and ALWAYS answered -- the name, "not yet", or "not to you"; the character sheet, with the ITEMISED why, because a hidden opinion score is the Phase 4 legibility failure again; and the ending, where he gives his verdict on you and, at nine Cores with the regard to match, the name he has been holding since OTA-236. The one model touch is a one-sentence brief (stance + tone) appended to the live Qwen persona prompt -- additive, so an empty brief leaves it byte-identical and a failed generation falls through exactly as before. A personality that exists only while a 0.5B model happens to be warm is not a personality; everything else is authored. 55 tests. |
| 4.28.99 -> 4.29.0 | 2026-08-03-1066 | OTA | PHASE 4 -- LET THE DEBT COME DUE, BEHIND A DIFFICULTY TOGGLE (twin of HAL 1089). MINOR bump: a new axis of the game, not another patch on the same one. THE TOGGLE (owner's call on the names and the rule): four tiers, Doom-shaped and first person, on a NEW FOURTH STEP of character creation after the motive -- "I only came for the salvage." / "I know what I owe." (DEFAULT) / "Let it come." / "Bury me with them." Each carries a subtitle saying plainly what changes, because a difficulty name that sounds good and explains nothing is a trap on a screen you cannot revisit. ⚠ LOWERABLE MID-RUN AND NEVER RAISABLE: you can always ask the buried country for less, and nobody finishes on "Bury me with them" a run they spent on salvage. It lives on the character sheet afterwards, with higher tiers dimmed rather than tappable so the rule is visible instead of enforced by a refusal. ⚠ WHY EVERYTHING IS NOT ON A DIAL: corruption and weather ALREADY bite (stat penalties, price markup, extra encounters, reposition cost, attack penalty, HP + corruption ticks) and that is shipped, played and balanced -- re-scaling it from a difficulty tier would put a multiplier on top of a year of tuning. So Phase 4 turns the two substrates that threaten NOTHING into pressure and only raises the RATE the other two accumulate: TIDE (time -> scarcity, +4% vendor prices per stage, ~4 in-game days per stage x dial, HARD CAP 6 stages / +24%, and exactly 1.0 for the whole of tier 1); HOSTILE (standing <= -25 and a faction starts FINDING you -- a gate on an interception that was already going to happen, capped at 22%, bounties exempt); CREEP (weather notches corruption faster); EXPOSURE (a storm bites harder). The last two sit AFTER title perks and BEFORE the race immunity at the weather choke point, so earned mitigations still work and a Sentinel is still immune. LEGIBILITY IS HALF THE FEATURE -- the quiet way this phase fails is pressure the player cannot see, so every tide stage crossing gets an authored line once (player.tideStageSeen). Every older save reads as 'owed': the game as it has always played plus the two new systems at their gentlest, with a one-tap way down. 27 tests. |
| 4.28.98 -> 4.28.99 | 2026-08-03-1065 | OTA | PHASE 3 -- MAKE THE STORY ASK QUESTIONS (twin of HAL 1088). Build plan verbatim: "the audience that pays premium for text is paying for consequence, and right now you have exactly one fork in the game... extend that shape: 1-2 genuine forks per motive, with lasting consequence. Chapter cards become decisions rather than broadcasts." WHAT MAKES THIS A FORK AND NOT WHAT WE HAD: The Missing's grave/lie/walker resolution is DEALT -- missingResolutionFor() hashes the identity seed -- so it proved the plumbing and the player never chose it. This is that plumbing with the hash replaced by a person. 10 forks, 29 options, 2 per motive, in app/data/story/forks.json. ⚠ FORKS ARE DERIVED, NOT QUEUED, and that is the whole answer to the risk the plan named ("the one place a save-migration bug would be unrecoverable for a player mid-arc"). A queued fork can be lost to a crash, a kill or a bad backfill, and a lost fork is a chapter of the player's story that silently never happens. dueFork(player) is a PURE read of the save: first fork whose motive matches, whose phase gate the run has passed, with no recorded answer. Kill the app in front of the card and it is due again on load; store.pendingFork is a VIEW and is safe to drop at any moment. The only persisted state is the ANSWER (player.storyChoices, forkId -> optionId): absent on every older save, which reads correctly as "asked nothing yet", so there is NO migration to get wrong; an id from a newer build is ignored rather than fatal; and answerFork writes the choice BEFORE paying the effects, so a death between the two costs coin, never a re-asked question. LASTING CONSEQUENCE IN THREE PLACES: (1) now -- an authored line plus coin / a keepsake / one-shot faction standing, keepsakes 'quest'-tagged so a decision cannot be pawned; (2) at the end -- one sentence per answer under WHAT YOU CHOSE on EndingScreen, permanently, beneath the motive epilogue that still closes the arc; (3) in the world -- TopicGate.requiresChoice lets the Phase 2 cast react, three authored (Tellin on the writ, Korash on the districts, Vesryn on the pages sent up early). "Chapter cards become decisions" is done by asking the instant the card is dismissed rather than making the card answerable -- a card holds nothing a fast tap can lose (OTA-1020) and a decision must hold exactly that -- so the fork overlay is the ONE modal in the game with no backdrop dismiss and no close button. Standing here is one-shot BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by a guard, the opposite of the two repeatable acts OTA-1064 had to meter. 25 tests. |

REPORTED NOT CHANGED: caught theft applies its standing loss on every attempt (same shape as 1, but what repeated stealing should cost is a design call); restitution banks only coin HANDED OVER, so buy-then-resell launders amends at the cost of the spread. 26 tests. |

A flourish is not more dialogue: the authored reply owns the words, the flourish owns the HANDS -- one short beat of what somebody is DOING while they answer. 55 authored lines in app/data/npcs/flourishes.json keyed two ways at once, by TRADE (10 buckets, so a smith's hands are not a scholar's; all 30 named vendors map) and by REGARD (6 rungs including wronged, so posture tracks the Phase 1 ledger); pools concatenate and index by a hash of person+topic, so it is deterministic like everything else since OTA-1049. ⚠ THE MODEL IS STILL NOT IN THE CRITICAL PATH. OTA-1058 built the conversation synchronous BECAUSE a 14-20s generation in front of a tapped topic is a loading screen, and nothing about that changed. The order is inverted instead: a request fires when the topic LIST OPENS (the one moment the player is reliably busy reading), the result sits in ONE module slot keyed to that person, and raising a topic TAKES from the slot or takes an authored line and cannot tell the difference. Slow model, dormant model and no model are all the same case -- an empty slot. No await anywhere in the exchange, no spinner, no degraded mode. The slot dies with the conversation and is never persisted. The judge for a generated line is pure and synchronous beside the brief that asks for it: must name the person, one sentence under 160 chars, terminated, no quotes/questions/first-person/second-person-opener (the two registers OTA-1031 caught) and no instruction echo (OTA-1030). Frequency: FIRST raise of a topic only (a re-tread gets the words back but not the business), max 3 per conversation, never the same line twice, silence rather than a repeat once spent. engine/flourish.ts carries the same no-await guard dialogue.ts carries; the test asserts both files plus that emitFlourish contains no await. 30 tests. |
| 4.28.95 -> 4.28.96 | 2026-08-03-1062 | OTA | THE NON-VENDOR CAST GETS A VOICE (twin of HAL 1085): 30 vendors were authored one at a time; the rest of the population cannot work that way and should not -- roadside traders are 24 procedurally-named people sharing two archetypes, wanderers are ARCHETYPES x FIRST_NAMES, escort leaders come from a name pool, Guardians are one voice per Capital. What makes them distinct is their KIND. Topic lookup now falls back to a CLASS entry (11 sets, 44 topics, 179 total) while the LEDGER still treats them as individuals -- Grit remembers you personally; what he says is what a roadside trader says. Exact id always wins, so authoring a specific person later needs no code change. AND THE VERB CAUGHT UP: talkToNpc only ever looked at currentScene.vendor because vendors were the only cast the ledger covered (OTA-1057 finished that and the verb had not been told); it now resolves vendor + wanderer + active escort leaders. Roadside and overlay traders arrive via the vendor slot, so 29 more people became talkable with no wiring. ⚠ class:guardian is authored but NOT yet reachable -- a Guardian is an enemy, so talking to one is the parley system's job; the content is written and gated, the door is open work. |

FIRE-ONCE off the SAME talkedTopics counter that drives "I have told you that one" (asked === 0 IS the first raise), so payout and acknowledgement read one fact and cannot drift. ⚠ NO STANDING, deliberately: OTA-803 deleted gifting over a faction-standing side door and OTA-1060 reopened it only behind a lifetime budget -- a topic granting standing would be a second door with no budget, and a test reads applyTopicGrant and fails if applyRepChange appears. Whispers from conversation close a real gap: the Yulka chain could previously only be planted by walking into the Mess and winning a 15% roll; now Tellin and Elara can just tell you. An already-held chain is skipped silently. Leads reuse pendingLead and never overwrite an unclaimed one. Everything that pays is gated behind 'familiar' or better, and a robbed shopkeeper grants nothing. |

What gifts really buy is the PERSONAL relationship -- one ledger row, that person's discount via regardPriceMult, and the memory -- which cannot cascade. Insults are NOT capped, or being rude becomes free once the budget is spent. Three more exploits closed: repeat gifts of the same item decay to nothing; anything under 12 TC is REFUSED and stays in the pack (accepting junk = emptying a bag at a cost of taps); gifts are consumed so the buy-back loop terminates. Reaction is driven by WHO THEY ARE, not price -- 9 authored preference sets plus a fallback, and something genuinely valuable is welcome from anybody. Deterministic. |

Costs -12 standing and a `wrongs` on their personal ledger -- visible in the Chronicle, +25% on everything they sell you, and payable off through OTA-1053's amends. ## Catch-up ledger — how 4.1.0 became 4.28.3 (2026-07-26)

MAJOR stayed **4**: same engine lineage since the AI-restructure promotion.
MINOR: 1 (at the freeze) + **27 significant feature waves** shipped between
OTA-603 and OTA-991 = **28**. PATCH: OTAs after the last wave closed (escorts,
OTA-989) → 990, 991, 992 = **3**. The waves, in ship order:

1. OTA-630–631 — instant Crucible forging (background naming)
2. OTA-636–637 — typed-damage combat rework (procs/DOTs, weak/resist gating)
3. OTA-642 — single-active mission system + auto-routing
4. OTA-643–646 — weapon coatings (+ armor resists)
5. OTA-650–655 — fusion picker + crafting-screen overhaul
6. OTA-668–673 — mission tracking + universal contract pause
7. OTA-675/690/707 — throwables & bandolier overhaul
8. OTA-691–692 — faction sigils
9. OTA-710–712 — gesture intent + provokable encounters
10. OTA-716–724/731 — found-only recipe discovery + Rare/Legendary expansion
11. OTA-726–730 — economy/gold-sink wave (paid services, premium stock)
12. OTA-~740–789 — the Hidden Market (square nav, all-faction brokering, rotation)
13. OTA-795 — dodge rework (opposed-contest AC-bypass gamble)
14. OTA-806–809 — social rework: parley + menace
15. OTA-815–819 — engaging-combat rework (scaling, packs, per-spawn weaknesses)
16. OTA-835 — race abilities become real mechanics
17. OTA-836–838 — visible-depth QoL (tap-to-explain, codex/bestiary)
18. OTA-843–845 — emergent depth (Chronicle, World Pulse, The Fallen)
19. OTA-847 — stealth system
20. OTA-849–868 — the LIVING WORLD war arc
21. OTA-869–870 — authored-questline content sprint (all 9 factions arced)
22. OTA-910–915 — the Great Climbs + Skyreacher drop + the Aetherkin
23. OTA-927–930 — the POWER rating + lens slot
24. OTA-947/959 — combat rebalance (defense de-runaway + legibility)
25. OTA-960–967 — loot audit + boss spoils table
26. OTA-973–978 — real-heights climbing arc + reach
27. OTA-985–989 — escort missions
